The expectation values parameterize the subhorizon evolution of the EFT modes before
t_{\Lambda}
. If the full theory is perturbative, then the subhorizon evolution is approximately Gaussian. Assuming the UV theory is given by
\lambda\phi^{4}
, then to
O(\lambda)
we can write

\displaystyle\big\langle\psi_{\rm EFT}(t_{\Lambda})\big|\varphi_{I,+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{1}\big)\,...\,\varphi_{I,+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{4}\big)\big|\psi_{\rm EFT}(t_{\Lambda})\big\rangle=\Big(\big\langle\varphi_{I,+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{1}\big)\varphi_{I,+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{2}\big)\big\rangle_{{\rm IC}^{(0)}}\big\langle\varphi_{I,+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{3}\big)\varphi_{I,+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{4}\big)\big\rangle_{{\rm IC}^{(0)}}

\displaystyle+2\big\langle\varphi_{I,+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{1}\big)\varphi_{I,+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{2}\big)\big\rangle_{{\rm IC}^{(0)}}\big\langle\varphi_{I,+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{3}\big)\varphi_{I,+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{4}\big)\big\rangle_{{\rm IC}^{(1)}}+{\rm perms}\Big)+\big\langle\varphi_{I,+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{1}\big)\,...\,\varphi_{I,+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{4}\big)\big\rangle_{{\rm IC}^{(1)}}\,,

where the final term on the RHS encodes the non-Gaussian contribution to the subhorizon evolution of the modes. There is a similar formula for the expectation value on the second line of
eq.

3.32
. However, since it is already multiplied by
c_{3,1}
, we only need the
\langle...\rangle_{\text{IC}^{(0)}}
contribution to the expectation value at this order.

The SdSET two point functions derived from the free theory are given in
eq.

3.18
. To include the impact of the UV interaction on the EFT, we compute
\big\langle\varphi_{I,+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{1}\big)\,...\,\varphi_{I,+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{4}\big)\big\rangle_{\rm IC}
and
c_{3,1}
by matching to the full theory. The superhorizon evolution between
t_{\Lambda}
and
t
generates the term proportional to
c_{3,1}
, and so we can isolate the initial conditions contribution by evaluating both sides of
eq.

3.32
at
t_{\Lambda}
, giving

\displaystyle\big\langle\varphi_{I,+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{1}\big)\,...\,\varphi_{I,+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{4}\big)\big\rangle_{\rm IC}=\big\langle\Omega\big|\phi\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{1},t_{\Lambda}\big)\,...\,\phi\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{4},t_{\Lambda}\big)\big|\Omega\big\rangle_{\rm connected}\,,

(3.34)

where the “connected” subscript refers to the fact that this does not include the contributions from products of lower point contractions.
The RHS of
eq.

3.34
can be computed using
eq.

3.4
, where
H_{I}
is given by the full theory interaction Hamiltonian and the time integrals extend from
-\infty
to
t_{\Lambda}
. Since the integral’s main region of support occurs when the modes are subhorizon, we cannot replace the mode functions with their late time behavior, and instead have to use their full UV form given in
eq.

3.3
. The UV mode functions simplify for scalars whose mass is much lighter than the Hubble constant and can be approximated by
eq.

3.8
.

One can then fix
c_{3,1}
by demanding that
eq.

3.32
reproduces the full theory prediction for the correlator in the regime
t>t_{\Lambda}
. While the split between subhorizon (
t<t_{\Lambda}
) and superhorizon (
t>t_{\Lambda}
) evolution is manifest in the EFT, this split has to be inputted by hand in the full theory, as was done in
eq.

3.29
; this is effectively making a choice of scheme. Practically, one can decompose the time integrals in
eq.

3.4
into regions before and after
t_{\Lambda}
. In our four point example, subhorizon contribution is already taken care of by the initial conditions, i.e.,
eq.

3.34
, while the second term in
eq.

3.32
must reproduce the superhorizon evolution. The Wilson coefficient
c_{3,1}
is fixed by this condition.

Since the split time
t_{\Lambda}
is arbitrary from the perspective of the full theory, all full-theory and EFT predictions of the
\phi
correlators must be independent of it. This provides an additional check on the matching calculation in the hard cutoff scheme. Fortunately, when we calculate with dynamical dim reg, the time integrals are manifestly independent of
t_{\Lambda}
, while still maintaining the split between initial conditions and time-evolution. In this sense, the hard-cutoff scheme proves the validity of dynamical dim reg, while dynamical dim reg makes it manifest that we can implement this procedure without breaking symmetries. This matches the more intuitive argument that our treatment of initial conditions and time evolution in SdSET is identical to the continuum EFT approach.

3.5
Stochastic Inflation from SdSET

From the point of view of our EFT, Stochastic Inflation can be understood as a consequence of operator mixing. Specifically, for light fields for which
\alpha\to 0
, the composite operators
\varphi_{+}^{n}
are degenerate to leading order (in that they have the same dimension as determined by the EFT power counting). Assuming the correlations of these fields are only due to the Gaussian contribution given in
eq.

3.18
, one encounters a UV divergence from a one-loop contraction

\displaystyle\big\langle\varphi_{+}^{n}({\vec{x}})\,...\,\big\rangle

\displaystyle\supset\big\langle\varphi_{+}^{n-2}({\vec{x}})\,...\,\big\rangle\times\binom{n}{2}\hskip 0.8pt\frac{C_{\alpha}^{2}}{2}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}p}{(2\hskip 0.8pt\pi)^{3}}\frac{H^{2-2\hskip 0.8pt\alpha}}{p^{3-2\hskip 0.8pt\alpha}}

\displaystyle\supset\big\langle\varphi_{+}^{n-2}({\vec{x}})\,...\,\big\rangle\times\binom{n}{2}\hskip 0.8pt\frac{C_{\alpha}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}\log[aH]\,.

(3.35)

The dynamical RG associated with this operator mixing can be written as

\frac{\partial}{\partial\texttt{t}}\big\langle\varphi_{+}^{n}({\vec{x}})\,...\,\big\rangle=\frac{n(n-1)}{8\hskip 0.8pt\pi^{2}}\hskip 0.8pt\big\langle\varphi_{+}^{n-2}({\vec{x}})\,...\,\big\rangle-\frac{n}{3}\sum_{m>1}\frac{c_{m,1}}{m!}\big\langle\varphi_{+}^{n-1}({\vec{x}})\hskip 0.8pt\varphi_{+}^{m}({\vec{x}})\,...\,\big\rangle\,,

(3.36)

where the second term arises from the classical time evolution. This equation contains the same information as Starobinsky’s formulation of Stochastic Inflation. Specifically, we can use the Fokker-Planck equation given in
eq.

2.2
to see that these two approaches are equivalent:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\texttt{t}}\big\langle\varphi_{+}^{n}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\texttt{t}}\int{\rm d}\varphi_{+}\varphi_{+}^{n}P(\varphi_{+},\texttt{t})

(3.37)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int{\rm d}\varphi_{+}\varphi_{+}^{n}\left(\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\varphi_{+}^{2}}P(\varphi_{+},\texttt{t})+\frac{1}{3}\frac{\partial}{\partial\varphi_{+}}\left[\sum_{m>1}\frac{c_{m,1}}{m!}\varphi_{+}^{m}P(\varphi_{+},\texttt{t})\right]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int{\rm d}\varphi_{+}\left(\frac{n(n-1)}{8\pi^{2}}\varphi_{+}^{n-2}P(\varphi_{+},\texttt{t})-\frac{n}{3}\varphi_{+}^{n-1}\sum_{m>1}\frac{c_{m,1}}{m!}\varphi_{+}^{m}P(\varphi_{+},\texttt{t})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n(n-1)}{8\pi^{2}}\big\langle\varphi_{+}^{n-2}\big\rangle-\frac{n}{3}\sum_{m>1}\frac{c_{m,1}}{m!}\big\langle\varphi_{+}^{n-1}\varphi_{+}^{m}\big\rangle\,,

where in the second line we plugged in
eq.

2.2
,
Ht=\texttt{t}
, the
\alpha=0
relation
\phi|_{\varphi_{-}=0}\to H\varphi_{+}
, and

V^{\prime}(\phi)\,\,\to\,\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\varphi_{-}}V(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})\Big|_{\varphi_{-}=0}=\sum_{m>1}\frac{c_{m,1}}{m!}\varphi_{+}^{m}\,.

(3.38)

This shows that
eq.

3.36
is equivalent to the leading order equation for Stochastic Inflation
eq.

2.2
. Therefore, calculating corrections to Stochastic Inflation has been reduced to the straightforward task of computing the higher order dynamical RG equation using SdSET. Concretely, we would expect to find mixing between composite operators
\varphi_{+}^{n}
and all possible
\varphi_{+}^{n^{\prime}}
such that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\texttt{t}}\big\langle\varphi_{+}^{n}\big\rangle=

\displaystyle-\frac{n}{3}\sum_{m>1}^{\text{odd}}\frac{c_{m,1}}{m!}\big\langle\varphi_{+}^{n+m-1}\big\rangle+\binom{n}{2}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}b_{m}\big\langle\varphi_{+}^{n+2m-2}\big\rangle

\displaystyle-\binom{n}{3}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}d_{m}\big\langle\varphi_{+}^{n+2m-2}\big\rangle+\binom{n}{4}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}e_{m}\big\langle\varphi_{+}^{n+2m-4}\big\rangle+...\ .

(3.39)

Note the role of the binomial coefficient which will originate from the number of fields inside
\varphi_{+}^{n}
whose contractions are responsible for mixing with a given operator, leading to a single log divergence. Repeating the above argument, we see that this dynamical RG is equivalent to

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\texttt{t}}P(\varphi_{+},\texttt{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\frac{\partial}{\partial\varphi_{+}}\left[\partial_{\varphi_{-}}V(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})|_{\varphi_{-}=0}P(\varphi_{+},\texttt{t})\right]+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\varphi_{+}^{2}}\left[\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{b_{m}}{2!}\varphi_{+}^{2m}P(\varphi_{+},\texttt{t})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.0pt+\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\varphi_{+}^{3}}\left(\varphi_{+}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{d_{m}}{3!}\varphi_{+}^{2m}P(\varphi_{+},\texttt{t})\right)+\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\varphi_{+}^{4}}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{e_{m}}{4!}\varphi_{+}^{2m}P(\varphi_{+},\texttt{t})\right)+\ldots\ ,

where we see that number of derivatives is related to the binomial coefficient of the associated mixing term. Comparing with
section

2.2
, we see that the NLO corrections are determined by
c_{5,1}
and
b_{1}
while the NNLO coefficients are
c_{7,1}
,
b_{2}
and
d_{0}
. Achieving NNLO accuracy requires matching
\lambda\phi^{4}
theory onto the SdSET at one loop, the subject of the next section. Note, however, that we have described Stochastic Inflation in terms of
\varphi_{+}
rather than the UV field
\phi
. This distinction will be important because the equations of Stochastic Inflation are not invariant under field redefintions. We will address these issues in detail in
section

5.3
. Finally, we note that this result demonstrates the Markovian assumption which led to
eq.

2.16
does in fact hold as a consequence of SdSET power counting. Specifically, the fact that the
\varphi_{-}
dynamics are irrelevant to the evolution of the
\varphi_{+}
correlators implies that the evolution of the system is indeed linear, and thus it has no “memory.”

4
Matching
\lambda\phi^{4}
Onto SdSET at One-Loop

In this section we will show how to match correlators of
\phi
to correlators of
\varphi_{+}
in the SdSET to determine the EFT parameters in terms of UV data. This then serves as input for any calculation of cosmological correlators for
\lambda\phi^{4}
theory in the long wavelength limit, including the corrections to Stochastic Inflation we will discuss in subsequent sections. Furthermore, by extending this program to one-loop order for the first time, this calculation will serve as a non-trivial check of the SdSET framework.

Unlike conventional EFTs, we match both the couplings of SdSET and the stochastic initial conditions, the former results from studying the time-dependent terms in the EFT while the latter is fixed at the time of horizon crossing. As a result, consistent matching of time-dependence of the UV correlators is non-trivial and requires that the SdSET is a complete representation of the long wavelength dynamics. In contrast, time-independent contributions to a given correlator can always be absorbed into the initial conditions (up to composite operators as discussed in
section

4.3
). As a result, for tree and one-loop matching we will be particularly focused on time-dependent UV contributions.

4.1
Tree-level Matching and Field Redefinitions

Tree-level matching in the interacting theory is non-trivial due to the impact on the initial conditions. We will need to introduce non-Gaussian initial conditions in order to match higher point correlation functions as calculated by the UV theory.

We can understand many important aspects of matching by Taylor expanding the UV calculation in the long wavelength limit. As a simple demonstration, we can explore the superhorizon behavior of the operator
\phi
. At first order in the coupling, we can apply the definition of the in-in correlator given in
eq.

3.4
with
Q(t)=\phi
, which gives

\displaystyle[\phi]_{\lambda}\big({\vec{x}},\tau\big)

\displaystyle=i\int^{\tau}{\rm d}\tau_{1}\Big[H_{\rm int}(\tau_{1}),\phi_{\rm in}\big({\vec{x}},\tau\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda}{3!}\int^{\tau}\frac{{\rm d}\tau_{1}}{(-H\tau_{1})^{4}}\int{\rm d}^{3}x_{1}\,\phi_{\rm int}({\vec{x}}_{1},\tau_{1})^{3}\,i\Big[\phi_{\rm int}\big({\vec{x}}_{1},\tau_{1}\big),\phi_{\rm int}\big({\vec{x}},\tau\big)\Big]\ ,

(4.1)

where
\phi_{\rm int}
are the interaction picture fields. Because the time integral runs over all times, this includes both the regime where the modes are hard (UV) and the long wavelength limit where the EFT applies.
Nevertheless, if we expand in the long wavelength limit and evaluate the integrals with dynamical dim reg, we will only get contributions from late times. Since
\phi_{\rm in}
are the free field operators, we can use the map given in
eq.

3.11
to determine the long wavelength behavior of the full theory:
3
3

3

Note we are not working in the EFT yet, because we have not integrated out the hard modes. All we are doing here is taking the long wavelength limit.

\displaystyle\phi_{\rm int}

\displaystyle\to H\big([aH]^{-\alpha}\varphi_{+}+[aH]^{-3+\alpha}\varphi_{-}\big)\,.

(4.2a)

Then using
\tau=-1/[aH]
and keeping only terms that survive as
[aH]\to\infty
, one finds

\displaystyle[\phi]_{\lambda}\big({\vec{x}},\texttt{t}\big)

\displaystyle\to\frac{\lambda}{3!}\,H\,\int^{\texttt{t}}{\rm d}\texttt{t}_{1}\int{\rm d}^{3}x_{1}\,[a(\texttt{t}_{1})H]^{-2\alpha}\varphi_{+}^{3}\big({\vec{x}}_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 40.0pt\ i\bigg(\Big[\varphi_{-}\big({\vec{x}}_{1}\big),\varphi_{+}\big({\vec{x}}\big)\Big]+\frac{[a(\texttt{t}_{1})H]^{3}}{[a(\texttt{t})H]^{3}}\Big[\varphi_{+}\big({\vec{x}}_{1}\big),\varphi_{-}\big({\vec{x}}\big)\Big]\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda}{3!}\frac{H}{3}\bigg(-\bigg(\!-\frac{1}{2\alpha}+\log[a(\texttt{t})H]\bigg)+\frac{1}{3}\bigg)\varphi_{+}^{3}\big({\vec{x}}\big)\,,

(4.3)

where in the last line we expanded in
\alpha\ll 1
. Note that the scaling dimension of
\varphi_{+}
is still
\alpha
and thus will provide the necessary distance scale to make the log dimensionless inside of a correlator, as is familiar from conventional dim reg.

Now we turn to exploring the same effect within the EFT direction by calculating the time evolution of
\varphi_{+}
using SdSET. Using
eq.

3.11
with
Q=\varphi_{+}
and
{\cal H}_{\rm int}=c_{3,1}\varphi_{+}^{3}\varphi_{-}/3!
we have

\displaystyle[\varphi_{+}]_{\lambda}({\vec{x}},\texttt{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{3,1}}{3!}\int^{\texttt{t}}{\rm d}\texttt{t}_{1}\int{\rm d}^{3}x_{1}[aH(\texttt{t}_{1})]^{-2\alpha}\varphi_{+}^{3}({\vec{x}}_{1})i\Big[\varphi_{-}\big({\vec{x}}_{1}\big),\varphi_{+}\big({\vec{x}}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{c_{3,1}}{3!}\frac{1}{3}\bigg(\!-\frac{1}{2\alpha}+\log[a(\texttt{t})H]\bigg)\varphi_{+}^{3}({\vec{x}})\,,

(4.4)

where it is trivial to match the tree-level UV interaction to the EFT interaction, such that

\displaystyle c_{4,0}=c_{3,1}=\,...\,=\lambda+O\big(\lambda^{2}\big)\,.

(4.5)

The equality between
c_{n,0}
and
c_{n-m,m}
found in matching is also fixed by the reparametrization invariance of SdSET. We see that the EFT is capturing the first term in the Taylor expansion of the UV theory given in
eq.

4.3
, but not the second.

The origin of the missing term is two-fold. First, we are only considering correlations of
\varphi_{+}
instead of the full UV field, which also includes
\varphi_{-}
. This alone would not matter, since
\varphi_{-}
is suppressed by
[aH]^{-3}
. However, in order to organize the interactions within the EFT, we removed the
c_{4,0}\varphi_{+}^{4}/4!
term by a field redefinition. Specifically, to remove the
c_{n,0}\varphi_{+}^{n}/n!
operator, we take

\varphi_{-}\to\varphi_{-}+\frac{c_{n,0}}{9(n-1)!}[aH]^{3-(n-1)\alpha}\varphi_{+}^{n-1}\ .

(4.6)

Therefore, keeping track of the field redefinition implies that we should use

\phi_{\rm EFT}\equiv{\underline{\varphi}}=H\left([aH]^{-\alpha}\varphi_{+}+[aH]^{-\beta}\varphi_{-}+\frac{c_{4,0}}{9}\frac{1}{3!}[aH]^{-3\alpha}\varphi_{+}^{3}+\,...\,\right)\,,

(4.7)

with
\alpha\to 0
and
\beta\to 3
. Now the quantities on the RHS live purely in the EFT. As a result
\varphi_{-}
will not contribute to correlation functions of
{\underline{\varphi}}
because they are suppressed by powers of
[aH]^{-3}
. Combining this with
eq.

4.4
, we find

\big[\,{\underline{\varphi}}\,\big]_{\lambda}=\varphi_{+}+\frac{1}{3!}\frac{H}{3}\bigg(c_{3,1}\bigg(\!\frac{1}{2\alpha}-\log[aH]\bigg)+\frac{c_{4,0}}{3}\bigg)\varphi_{+}^{3}({\vec{x}})\,,

(4.8)

where we dropped terms suppressed by powers of
[aH]
. Now we see that this matches the UV expression in
eq.

4.3
when we use the tree-level matching relations
c_{3,1}=c_{4,0}=\lambda
given in
eq.

4.5
.

This result also provides the map between SdSET and Refs.
[
31
,
35
]
, which derived the soft behavior by explicitly expanding the UV in-in correlator in the superhorizon limit. The tree-like structure they observe is a consequence of our power counting as only
c_{n,1}
is marginal and hence interactions only include a single factor of
\varphi_{-}
. The nested set of commutators in
eq.

3.4
ensures that the marginal operators always have a tree-like structure. In SdSET, this is manifest from dynamical RG, and all the additional finite terms arise from the field redefinitions.

By similar considerations, we must apply the field redefinition to match the UV potential onto
V(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})
in the EFT.
Although
\lambda\varphi_{+}^{4}
has been removed, this procedure introduces higher order terms, such as

\displaystyle\frac{c_{3,1}}{3!}\varphi_{+}^{3}\varphi_{-}

\displaystyle\,\,\to\,\,\frac{c_{3,1}\,c_{4,0}}{9(3!)^{2}}[aH]^{3}\varphi_{+}^{6}

(4.9a)

\displaystyle\frac{c_{2,2}}{4}[aH]^{-3}\varphi_{+}^{2}\varphi_{-}^{2}

\displaystyle\,\,\to\,\,\frac{c_{2,2}\,c_{4,0}}{18(3!)}\varphi_{+}^{5}\varphi_{-}\,.

(4.9b)

Removing the first term will introduce a
\varphi_{+}^{7}\varphi_{-}
interaction at order
\lambda^{3}
and so on. As a result, our field redefinition requires that

V(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})\supset\frac{1}{3!}\varphi_{-}\left(c_{3,1}\varphi_{+}^{3}+\frac{c_{2,2}c_{4,0}}{18}\varphi_{+}^{5}+\frac{c_{2,2}c_{3,1}c_{4,0}}{162}\varphi_{+}^{7}+\,...\,\right)\ .

(4.10)

Finally, using
c_{2,2}=c_{3,1}=c_{4,0}=\lambda
from matching, we arrive at

V(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})\supset\frac{\lambda}{3!}\varphi_{-}\left(\varphi_{+}^{3}+\frac{\lambda}{18}\varphi_{+}^{5}+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{162}\varphi_{+}^{7}+\,...\,\right)\,.

(4.11)

or
c_{5,1}=\frac{\lambda^{2}}{18}\frac{5!}{3!}
and
c_{7,1}=\frac{\lambda^{3}}{162}\frac{7!}{(3!)}
.

The correction to
c_{5,1}
is equivalent to the NLO corrections to the effective potential calculated in Refs.
[
30
]
and
[
34
]
using complementary techniques. While these two references approach this problem from different perspectives, the wavefunction of the universe and the dS static patch respectively, both effectively integrate out the decaying mode
\varphi_{-}
which leads to an additional term in the potential. Instead, when
\varphi_{-}
is included, our corrections arise from insuring
\varphi_{-}
does not mix with
\varphi_{+}
at higher orders in perturbation theory. As the dimensions of
\varphi_{+}
and
\varphi_{-}
are well separate for
\alpha\to 0
, removing mixing can always be achieved by such a field redefinition. Additionally, it is easy to determine the correction to
c_{2n+1,1}\propto\lambda^{n}
by repeated application of
eq.

4.6
. Most importantly, we do not integrate out
\varphi_{-}
to ensure we have a local action, rather than an open EFT for the growing mode alone
[
29
]
.

What we have accomplished thus far is to determine the correct basis of operators to match the EFT and UV descriptions. We have ensured that the superhorizon limit of
\phi
and
{\underline{\varphi}}
agree as operators at higher orders in
\lambda
. However, in order to match the correlators of the UV theory, which include the subhorizon evolution, we will need to determine the stochastic initial conditions beyond the Gaussian limit.

In order to correctly match the four-point function, we write the EFT trispectrum to order
\lambda
as
4
4

4

For convenience, we take
C_{\alpha}\to 1
for the light fields and have dropped the additional constants that arise from expanding
C_{\alpha}=1+\alpha(\gamma_{E}-2+\log 2)+{O}\big(\alpha^{2}\big)
. While this choice has no impact on the physics (since the correct constants will appear in the initial conditions by matching), it will simplify the algebra significantly.

\displaystyle\Big\langle{\underline{\varphi}}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{1}\big){\underline{\varphi}}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{2}\big){\underline{\varphi}}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{3}\big){\underline{\varphi}}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{4}\big)\Big\rangle_{C}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\Big\langle\big[\,{\underline{\varphi}}\,\big]_{\lambda}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{1}\big)\varphi_{+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{2}\big)\varphi_{+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{3}\big)\varphi_{+}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{4}\big)\Big\rangle^{\prime}_{C}+{\rm permutations}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.0pt+\Big\langle{\underline{\varphi}}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{1}\big){\underline{\varphi}}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{2}\big){\underline{\varphi}}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{3}\big){\underline{\varphi}}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{4}\big)\Big\rangle^{\prime}_{{\rm IC}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda\,H^{4}}{8(k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4})^{3}}\sum_{i}\frac{k_{i}^{3}}{3}\left(c_{3,1}\bigg(\!\frac{1}{2\alpha}+\log\frac{k_{i}}{[aH]}\bigg)+\frac{c_{4,0}}{3}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.0pt+\Big\langle{\underline{\varphi}}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{1}\big){\underline{\varphi}}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{2}\big){\underline{\varphi}}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{3}\big){\underline{\varphi}}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{4}\big)\Big\rangle^{\prime}_{{\rm IC}^{(1)}}\,,

(4.12)

where
c_{3,1}=c_{4,0}=\lambda
as before, and the subscript “
C
” denotes that this is only the connected contributions. Matching this EFT expression to the UV result in
eq.

3.9
fixes the non-Gaussian contribution to the initial conditions:

\displaystyle\Big\langle{\underline{\varphi}}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{1}\big){\underline{\varphi}}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{2}\big){\underline{\varphi}}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{3}\big){\underline{\varphi}}\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{4}\big)\Big\rangle^{\prime}_{{\rm IC}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda\,H^{4}}{8(k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4})^{3}}\Bigg[\frac{\sum_{i}k_{i}^{3}\big(-\frac{1}{2\alpha}+\gamma_{E}-2+\log\frac{k_{t}}{k_{i}}\big)}{3}

\displaystyle-\frac{k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4}}{k_{t}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{9}k_{t}^{3}+2\sum_{h<i<j}k_{h}k_{i}k_{j}+\frac{1}{3}k_{t}\Big(\sum k_{i}^{2}-\sum_{i<j}k_{i}k_{j}\Big)\Bigg]\,,

(4.13)

where
k_{t}=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}
. Most significantly, all the time-dependence of the full UV trispectrum is already captured by the EFT and, as expected, the initial conditions are only required for matching the time-independence contributions. This result is extended to the six-point function in
appendix

A
as expected from general arguments.

Matching Derivative Operators:
Before moving on the loop-level matching, let us briefly comment on the case where the UV theory is itself an effective theory. Specifically, we are only considering the case of a
\lambda\phi^{4}
interaction in the UV, while in principle there could be a variety of higher derivative (irrelevant) interactions as well. The first such operator we can write down is
(\nabla_{\mu}\phi\nabla^{\mu}\phi)^{2}/M^{4}
. The full tree level trispectrum was calculated in Ref.
[
56
]
and is given by

\displaystyle\hskip-1.0pt\Big\langle\phi\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{1}\big)\phi\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{2}\big)\phi\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{3}\big)\phi\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{4}\big)\Big\rangle_{\nabla^{4}}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{3}\delta\left(\sum_{i}\vec{k}_{i}\right)\frac{1}{M^{4}}\frac{H^{8}}{\prod_{i}2k_{i}^{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.0pt\times\Bigg[-\frac{144k_{1}^{2}k_{2}^{2}k_{3}^{2}k_{4}^{2}}{k_{t}^{5}}-4\Bigg(\frac{12k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4}}{k_{t}^{5}}+\frac{3\prod_{i<j<l}k_{i}k_{j}k_{l}}{k_{t}^{4}}

\displaystyle\hskip 40.0pt+\frac{\prod_{i<j}k_{i}k_{j}}{k_{t}^{3}}+\frac{1}{k_{t}}\Bigg)\left(\left(\vec{k}_{1}\cdot\vec{k}_{2}\right)\left(\vec{k}_{3}\cdot\vec{k}_{4}\right)+2\text{ perms}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 40.0pt+\left(\vec{k}_{1}\cdot\vec{k}_{2}\right)\left(\frac{4k_{3}^{2}k_{4}^{2}}{k_{t}^{3}}+\frac{12\left(k_{1}+k_{2}\right)k_{3}^{2}k_{4}^{2}}{k_{t}^{4}}+\frac{48k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}^{2}k_{4}^{2}}{k_{t}^{5}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 40.0pt+\text{5 perms}\Bigg]\,.

(4.14)

What we notice right away is that this gives us a completely time-independent result. It can therefore only be absorbed into the initial conditions of
\varphi_{+}
:

\displaystyle\Big\langle\varphi_{+}\big(\vec{k}_{1}\big)\varphi_{+}\big(\vec{k}_{2}\big)\varphi_{+}\big(\vec{k}_{3}\big)\varphi_{+}\big(\vec{k}_{4}\big)\Big\rangle_{\rm IC}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle H^{-4}\Big\langle\phi\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{1}\big)\phi\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{2}\big)\phi\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{3}\big)\phi\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{4}\big)\Big\rangle_{\nabla^{4}}\,.

(4.15)

This result illustrates a broader feature of physics in dS: higher derivatives decouple at long wavelength and thus contribute, at most, time-independent correlation functions. This property is made manifest in SdSET and must hold in matching. As a result, a variety of possible UV theories only impact the initial conditions and not the long wavelength dynamics. In this sense, our choice to focus
\lambda\phi^{4}
is not missing more complicated superhorizon evolution. Instead, higher derivative terms are trivially matched in SdSET and thus do not further illuminate the structure of the EFT.

4.2
One-loop Matching

When calculating loops in the UV theory, rather than the EFT, our EFT regulator (dynamical dim reg) is not effective.
5
5

5

In a forthcoming paper
[
57
]
, it will be shown that when these integrals are transformed to Mellin space, then it is indeed possible to implement dynamical dim reg in the full theory.
Loop calculations in the UV are not scaleless and therefore we will need to regulate them differently than the EFT approach. This difference will be absorbed into the matching calculating. In
appendix

B
, we match using a hard cutoff in both theories for an example with consistent regulators in both and find identical results, up to scheme dependent coefficients. To avoid the usual challenges of working with a hard cutoff, we will use dimensional regularization in the UV theory via

\Big\langle\phi\big({\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}},\tau\big)\,\phi\big(\!-\!{\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}},\tau^{\prime}\big)\Big\rangle=\frac{\pi}{4}H^{d-1}(-\tau)^{\frac{d}{2}}\left(-\tau^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\text{H}_{\nu}\big(\!-\!k\tau\big)\text{H}_{\nu}^{\star}\big(\!-\!k\tau^{\prime}\big)\,,

(4.16)

where
\nu=\sqrt{d^{2}/4-m^{2}/H^{2}}
. To simplify calculations, we can fix
\nu=3/2
for any dimension, and then can regulate integrals that appear via an analytic continuation in
d
. We note that while this will regulate the one-loop divergences that appear in this section, dim reg alone is not sufficient in general, as we will see below.

We will begin with the one-loop power spectrum of the growing mode, illustrated in the left side of
fig.

2
. In the UV theory, a standard in-in calculations gives us

\displaystyle\Big\langle\phi\big(\hskip 0.8pt{\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}\,\big)\phi\big(\hskip 0.8pt{\!\!\vec{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{584904}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\,\,k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{584904}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\,\,k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{731706}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\,\,k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{951907}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\,\,k$}}}}}}^{\hskip 0.8pt\prime}}}\hskip 0.8pt\big)\Big\rangle^{\prime}_{(1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda}{4k^{3}}\frac{H^{2}}{3}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+\log\frac{2k}{[aH]}+\gamma_{E}-2\right)\bigg[[aH]^{-\epsilon}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{d}p}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{1}{2p^{3}}\bigg]\,.

(4.17)

Regulating the IR by substituting
p^{2}\to p^{2}+K^{2}
in the denominator, we get

\displaystyle\Big\langle\phi\big(\hskip 0.8pt{\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}\,\big)\phi\big(\hskip 0.8pt{\!\!\vec{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{584904}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\,\,k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{584904}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\,\,k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{731706}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\,\,k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{951907}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\,\,k$}}}}}}^{\hskip 0.8pt\prime}}}\hskip 0.8pt\big)\Big\rangle^{\prime}_{(1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda}{8\pi^{2}k^{3}}\frac{H^{2}}{3}\bigg(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+\log\frac{2k}{[aH]}+\gamma_{E}-2\bigg)\bigg[\frac{1}{\epsilon}-\log\frac{K}{[aH]}+\frac{1}{2}\big(\log 4\pi-\gamma_{E}\big)\bigg]\,.

This is the complete one-loop power spectrum of the UV theory.

{\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}
{\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}\,^{\prime}
t_{1}
{\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{1}
{\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{2}
{\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{3}
{\vec{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{364708}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{417140}{458752}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{511514}{458751}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}}}}_{4}
t_{1}
